# Orbit V1 — finish line and progress tracker

**This file is the only source of truth for V1 progress.** Whoever works on V1 reads it
first and updates it last. A step is done only when its box is ticked here **with its
evidence** (PR link, commit, or the check's output pasted under the step). If this file
and anything else disagree — memory, a chat summary, an old plan — this file wins and the
other thing gets fixed.

Owner: the project owner. Driver: the operating agent (full authority: commits, merges, verification,
changes on the test box). Started 2026-10-04 from core v0.32.1.

## 1. The finish line

V1 is done when all three are true:

1. **Onboarding.** On a bare Hetzner server, a fresh agent using `orbit-onboard` brings up an
   instance to a green `make verify` from **one input form given at the start** plus the two
   GitHub App clicks (create, install), with **no supervision after that**. Human time ≤ ~10
   minutes. Proven by rebuilding the test box from bare (M4).
2. **Operating.** On that instance, a fresh agent holding only orbitd (MCP) and the skills
   passes every scenario in §5 and §6 against the test tenant `checkers-lab`.
3. **Proof.** Every passing scenario is a regression stage in `make verify` (or an offline
   test in the local suite, `scripts/local-tests.sh`), and the docs plus the measured sizing
   describe the system that passed.
4. **Lean and opt-in.** Every optional platform part is a feature an organization chooses in
   onboarding and switches later with one command, with a status view and the matching MCP
   tools appearing and disappearing (M2c); the minimal platform runs on a small box.
5. **Auto-fitting.** Service resources are fitted automatically, opt-in, and verified to fail
   safe (M4b, last before the release).

Orbit V1 is a DevOps platform an agent can **operate**. Agents acting on their own are V2.

## 2. Rules for the whole run

- **Agents operate Orbit; no human reviews a PR, ever** (owner, 2026-10-05). A PR is a record,
  not a check. Every safeguard that matters is code that runs without a person: orbitd's roles,
  confirmations and ledger, the commit gate (M2c), the local test suite, `make verify`.
- **No CI. Everything runs on the operator machine** (owner, 2026-10-06: "no CI ... you need to
  verify something? run it on this machine. That's a rule"). Nothing runs in GitHub Actions: no
  workflow in core, none in the template. The offline tests run on the laptop
  (`scripts/local-tests.sh`, a Linux container with the desk's pinned tools): the files a change
  touches before it is merged, the whole suite before a release is tagged, the tally pasted.
- **New orbitd tools are added when the work shows a real, repeated need** (owner, 2026-10-05:
  "the goal is ease of use for YOU"). Each new tool goes behind roles, the confirmation round where
  it mutates, and the ledger, and its need is recorded in §8. Approved so far: M2c's list. Doing
  through desk scripts what a tool should do (skipping the guardrails) is a gap to close, not a
  workaround to keep.
- **Builds run on the operator machine**: `docker buildx build` + push to
  `ghcr.io/<owner>/<image>:<branch>-<sha7>`, and only from a clean working tree whose commit
  is already pushed. No GitHub Actions anywhere.
- **Every code change lands through a PR**, merged by the driver once its local tests pass
  (the tally in the PR). Related fixes go in one PR. Evidence written into this file goes
  straight to `main` (owner, 2026-10-06); nothing else is pushed to `main` directly.
- **Proof is written before the work, and verification only grows** (owner, 2026-10-05:
  "verification is a big part of all of this"). Every open step in §4 carries a `Proof required`
  block: the checks that tick it and what their output must show. A step is not started without
  one. The driver adds a proof wherever it sees a gap (a new failure mode, a path no check
  covers) and records it under the step; a proof is never removed or weakened. Each proof has
  three layers where they apply: an offline test in the local suite that fails without the change, a live
  check on the test box with its output pasted, and a fresh-agent scenario.
- **Never weaken a check to make it pass.** A test or verify-stage change goes in its own
  commit whose message says why. A check that cannot run is not a pass.
- **Evidence or it didn't happen** (METHOD.md): each claim here is CONFIRMED (a command and
  its output) or marked PLAUSIBLE.
- **Hard stops — ask the owner, do not proceed:** spending money, DNS changes outside the
  test domain's existing records, deleting data that has no verified backup, anything that
  touches root keys or the Recovery Kit, anything on a machine other than the test box.

## 3. Environment

| What | Where |
|---|---|
| Core repo | `orbit-core` → `~/source/repos/orbit-core` |
| Instance repo (test box) | `example-org/example-platform` → `~/source/repos/example-platform` |
| Test tenant source | `owner-org/checkers-api` (.NET 8, Postgres via Npgsql, prometheus-net) → clone into `example-org/checkers-lab` (private, history kept) |
| Test box | Hetzner project "example", node `example-node-1`, 203.0.113.65, 15 GiB, Ubuntu 26.04, k3s v1.36. `ssh orbit` from the operator laptop (key `~/.ssh/orbit-claude`). kubectl runs on the box: `ssh orbit kubectl …` |
| Domain | `example.com` (prod), `*.dev.example.com` (dev). Grafana at `grafana.`, orbitd at `orbit.` |
| Alerts | Alertmanager → Telegram (already reaches the owner) |
| Operator machine | the owner's Windows laptop: Docker Desktop (start it), `gh` logged in as the owner's account (needs the `write:packages` scope for GHCR pushes) |
| Desk commands on the box | `orbit platform plan|apply` run from the orbitd image in a one-off pod with the node kubeconfig (see the 2026-10-04 log entry) |

State at start (CONFIRMED 2026-10-04): platform matches git (`orbit platform plan` empty
after orbitd was bumped to v0.32.1); tenant apps `esc-*` still running; platform memory
~1.2–1.5 GiB RSS.

### M2b — Observability suite (§6)
- [x] Every item in §6 passes, wired into a verify stage; alert rules have offline `promtool test rules` in CI.
  - PR #199 (merged): every platform alert rule (14) has a promtool case — firing, and not firing where
    scoping is the point — run in CI by scripts/test_rules_promtool.py, which refuses an alert with no
    case and proves the cases bite against two broken rules. New rules for classes §6 names that the
    platform lacked: OrbitDatabaseDown, OrbitIngressLatencyHigh, OrbitLogsNotArriving,
    OrbitObjectStoreFailing (node memory and alert delivery are kube-prometheus-stack defaults).
  - **Proof required:** the six §6 drivers each end `N passed, 0 failed` on one core release, pasted:
    signals, alerts-e2e, watchers, retention, load, security; the dashboards check returns every panel
    with data; `make verify` is green afterwards (the drivers leave nothing behind).
    Status 2026-10-05 on v0.33.9 (all six on one release, after #230 and #232): signals 6/0,
    security 7/0, retention 11/0, load 4/0, watchers 18/0, alerts-e2e 43/0; the dashboards check
    returns 13 prod and 12 dev panels, every one `ok`; `make verify` afterwards 25/412/10/84/3/19 with
    0 failed. The outputs are pasted at the end of this row. What was wrong on v0.33.7 and fixed:
    - watchers "bucket unreachable": the fault restarted Loki (an /etc/hosts alias), and Loki cannot
      start without its bucket, so no object-store metric existed. Measured with the bucket's address
      blocked while Loki ran: no status is counted for the first minutes, then 500s. The rule gains
      "no successful upload in 15m while lines arrive" (promtool case fails on the old rule); the
      driver blocks the bucket with an egress NetworkPolicy instead.
    - load "too many series": the alert works (run by hand: target refused, `up`=0, counter rose,
      alert fired after 5m at 15:00:48Z); in the driver run the burst pod still held the namespace's
      pod quota, so the test pod never started. The driver must delete the burst pod first and fail
      loudly if its target is not running.
    - load "burst count": the count query returned no JSON; it moves to the shared `lokiq` helper.
    Evidence (2026-10-05, test box, core v0.33.9; all six drivers on that one release):

### M2c — Opt-in features, the commit gate, guardrail tools (designed 2026-10-05, approved)
Goal: the platform's optional parts are features (one registry in core: what each renders, requires,
costs and which MCP tools it owns); minimal by default; switched by one command from the CLI, an
agent or a future UI, all through orbitd; status for each; tools follow the features live.
- [x] Features registry (`features.yaml` in core): core (always), logs (needs a bucket), traces (needs
  a bucket), dashboards, uptime, backups (needs a bucket), databases, alerts-telegram, fit. Desired
  state under `features:` in `instance/config.yaml`; instance-init renders only enabled features;
  shared consumers follow (Grafana datasources, tenant `Instrumentation`, Traefik tracing, the deploy
  step's log assertion).
  - **Proof required:** (1) Offline: for each feature, a test renders the instance with it off and on: off, none of
    its files exist and `make check` passes; on, all exist. A test fails if any platform file belongs
    to no feature or to two, and if the registry names a tool, app or verify tag that does not exist.
    (2) Live: on the box, each optional feature is switched off then on; for each, pasted: the
    namespaces and workloads before and after, and platform RSS before and after (the saving is
    measured, not estimated). (3) The minimal profile (every optional feature off) runs for 6 h (owner, 2026-10-06; it was 24 h)
    with `make verify` green and its platform RSS pasted; PRODUCT.md's sizing table gets that row.
  - Progress (PR "M2c: the features registry; logs and traces are features"): the registry is
    `provisioning/features.yaml` (vendored with the staged tree it describes), read by one module,
    `scripts/verify/orbit_features.py`, that the scaffolder, the generator and the verify stage all
    call. A feature owns whole files (its `paths`, removed from the tree when it is off) and marked
    blocks inside other features' files (`# orbit:feature <name>` ... `# orbit:end <name>`: Grafana's
    Loki and Tempo datasources and their links, Traefik's trace export, the alloy scrape, the log
    and trace alert rules). No `features:` block reads as before features existed (on wherever the
    needs are met), so an upgrade changes nothing; with a block, only what is set `true` is on.
    Verify rows key on the feature's own name (`FEATURE_<NAME>`, held to the registry by a test)
    rather than on a separate tag. Done so far: core, logs, traces. Still to do in this row:
    dashboards, uptime, databases, backups, alerts-telegram; the live switch-off/on with measured
    memory; the 24 h minimal run.
  - Progress 2026-10-06 (#240, in v0.34.0-rc5): dashboards, uptime, databases, backups and
    alerts-telegram are registry entries; every reader follows them (scaffolder, generator, stage5,
    orbitd). What still needs a feature that is off is refused before anything is written (a project
    database, a probe monitor, `monitoring.probes`, a monitor that notifies telegram). Offline proof (1):
    `scripts/test_features_registry.py` (each feature off and on over a real scaffold, no file unowned
    or owned twice, every tool and app real), `scripts/test_catalog_features.py`.
    Live proof (2), on the test box at rc5, each switched with `orbit_features_set` (logs and traces
    were switched on rc3 and rc4: 1514.1 -> 1199.5 MiB with traces off). Platform RSS is the sum of
    container RSS outside the tenant namespaces; the two public doors were asked after each switch:

- [x] `orbit_features_list` (viewer) and `orbit_features_set` (admin, confirmation, ledger): validate
  requirements (logs without a bucket is refused, saying so), commit through orbitd's git job, apply
  or retire the feature's apps in-cluster (only the apps the registry lists), wait ready, report.
  `orbit features list|status|enable|disable` on the desk calls the same tool; `--local` for
  bootstrap and recovery; `GET /api/features` for a future UI.
  - **Proof required:** (1) Offline: a viewer or operator token is refused `orbit_features_set`; an admin call
    without the confirmation round changes nothing; a replayed confirmation is refused; enabling logs
    or traces with no bucket is refused with a sentence naming the bucket; each accepted change writes
    one ledger entry. (2) Live scenario S19, fresh admin agent: "turn traces off, then on again":
    checks that the apps are gone then back and Ready, the ledger holds both changes, bucket data from
    before is still queryable after re-enabling, and nothing else in the cluster changed
    (`orbit platform plan` empty). (3) `orbit features enable|disable` on the desk produces the same
    ledger entries as the tool; `--local` works with orbitd scaled to zero. (4) A change interrupted
    mid-apply (orbitd restarted during it) converges on the next tick and never leaves a half-enabled
    feature reported as on.
  - Progress (PR "M2c: switching a feature"): `orbit_features_set` and `orbit_features_list` exist,
    with `orbit features list|enable|disable` (through orbitd) and `orbit features
    status|set|converge` (the desk's own path, which works with orbitd down) and `GET /api/features`.
    How a switch runs: (1) validate against the registry and the checkout's config; (2) confirm,
    naming every Application applied or retired; (3) `scripts/features-commit.sh` rewrites the
    `features:` block, re-renders the tree and lands ONE commit with trailers `Orbit-Action:
    features-set`, `Orbit-Actor`, `Orbit-Role: admin`, `Orbit-Feature: <name>=<on|off>` (the ledger
    entry); (4) wait for the checkout; (5) a Kubernetes Job in `orbit` runs `orbit features converge`
    at that commit: apply the feature's Applications, re-apply with prune the Applications that
    change with it (its `consumers`), retire the Applications of a feature turned off, wait ready,
    record what was converged (ConfigMap `orbit/orbit-features`). The engine is idempotent and the
    reconcile loop starts a converge whenever the cluster is behind the checkout (backing off 10
    minutes doubling to an hour at one commit), which is what makes an interrupted change converge.
    Offline proof (1) is `tests/test_features_tools.py` (role refusal for viewer and operator;
    nothing changes without the confirmation round; a confirmation cannot be replayed for another
    switch or by another admin; logs and traces without a bucket refused naming the bucket) and
    `scripts/test_features_commit.py` (one commit with the trailers; off then on restores the tree
    exactly). Live proofs (2) (3) (4) are still to run.
  - Progress 2026-10-06, live on the test box. Proof (4), a switch interrupted mid-apply (v0.34.0-rc7):
    traces turned off with the tool; while the converge Job was applying, the Job was deleted and
    orbitd restarted. The state orbitd reported, every 40 s, until the cluster matched again:

- [x] MCP tools follow the features: `tools/list` filtered by role AND enabled features; orbitd declares
  `tools.listChanged` and sends a debounced `notifications/tools/list_changed` when a commit changes
  the set; a call to a disabled feature's tool gets `isError` with the command that enables it.
  `orbit_logs_query` answers from the Kubernetes API (live and previous containers) when logs are off.
  - **Proof required:** (1) Offline: `tools/list` for each role with each feature on and off equals the registry's
    expectation, for every feature (a table-driven test over the registry, so a new feature is covered
    without a new test). (2) A real MCP client holds a session open while a feature is switched: it
    receives `notifications/tools/list_changed` within one reconcile period and its next `tools/list`
    differs exactly by that feature's tools; pasted for both protocol revisions orbitd serves.
    (3) A call to a just-disabled tool returns `isError` with the enabling command, never a
    connection error or a traceback. (4) Scenario S20, fresh viewer agent with traces off, asked for a
    trace: it reports the feature is off and how to enable it, and does not invent data. (5) Logs
    fallback: S06d and S08b re-run with logs off and pass (the agent names the cause from Kubernetes
    logs); the ship, rollback and restart tools pass with logs off (their log assertion keys off).
    (6) Switching ten times in a minute produces a bounded number of notifications (debounce).
  - Progress (PR "M2c: MCP tools follow the features"): orbitd reads the registry and the
    `features:` block from its instance checkout through the same module as the scaffolder
    (`orbitd/orbit_features.py`, held byte-equal to `scripts/verify/orbit_features.py` by a test).
    The role gate drops a disabled feature's tools from `tools/list` and answers a call to one with
    `isError` code `FEATURE_OFF` naming the feature and how to turn it on (role is checked first).
    A watch loop re-reads the checkout every 10 s and publishes one `tools/list_changed` on the
    SDK's `subscriptions/listen` bus when the hidden set changed (10 flips inside a period: one
    notification, or none when it ends where it started). `orbit_logs_query` and the deploy step's
    log assertion read the node's pod logs through the Kubernetes API when logs is off (current
    and previous container runs, bounded, line filters only).
    Offline proofs (1), (3), (6) and the offline half of (2) and (5) are in
    `tests/test_features_gate.py`, `tests/test_features_notify.py` (a real server process and a
    real SDK client over a socket) and `tests/test_logs_without_loki.py`.
    Finding to settle before (2) is ticked: orbitd serves stateless HTTP, so only a 2026-07-28
    client (which opens `subscriptions/listen`) can be told; a handshake-era (2025-06-18) request
    has no standing stream to be told on and sees the change at its next `tools/list`. The live
    check must show what each real host does (Claude Code direct, and through the confirm proxy,
    which holds a stdio channel to its host and so can forward the notification).
  - Progress 2026-10-06, live on the test box (v0.34.0-rc7). Proof (2): two real clients held a
    session while traces was switched off (the tool was called at 06:04:06Z) and on (06:08:15Z): the
    SDK client of the 2026-07-28 revision with `subscriptions/listen` open, and a plain JSON-RPC
    client of the 2025-06-18 revision asking `tools/list` every 5 s with its GET stream held open:
    ```
    06:03:48Z [2025-06-18 raw client] initialize: protocolVersion=2025-06-18 tools capability={"listChanged": false}
    06:03:48Z [2025-06-18 raw client] standing GET stream open: HTTP 200
    06:03:48Z [2025-06-18 raw client] GET for a standing stream of server messages: HTTP 200 (this revision learns of a change at its next tools/list)
    06:03:49Z [2026-07-28 SDK client] connected; 24 tools; opening subscriptions/listen
    06:03:49Z [2026-07-28 SDK client] listening; server honors tools_list_changed=True
    06:03:51Z [2025-06-18 raw client] tools/list: 24 tools
    06:04:34Z [2025-06-18 raw client] SAW the change at its next tools/list: 22 tools, removed ['orbit_traces_find', 'orbit_traces_get'] added []
    06:04:37Z [2026-07-28 SDK client] TOLD ToolsListChanged; next tools/list: 22 tools, removed ['orbit_traces_find', 'orbit_traces_get'] added []
    06:06:44Z [2026-07-28 SDK client] connection lost (ExceptionGroup); reconnecting in 5 s
    06:06:44Z [2025-06-18 raw client, GET stream] connection lost (RemoteProtocolError); reconnecting in 5 s
    06:06:50Z [2025-06-18 raw client] standing GET stream open: HTTP 200
    ```

- [x] The commit gate: before applying a new instance commit orbitd runs a chain of checks; a
  failing commit is refused, the last good commit stays applied, the refusal goes to the ledger,
  `orbit_deploy_status` and an `OrbitCommitRefused` alert. Built as a chain of independent checks
  (owner, 2026-10-05): each check is one small module with one shape (in: the commit's checkout;
  out: pass, or fail with one plain sentence), run in the order of one list; the first failure
  refuses the commit. Adding a check = one module + one line in the list. Each check is also a
  desk command (`orbit check <name>`, `orbit check all` = the whole chain), so agents run the same
  chain before merging and the box and the laptop can never disagree. First checks: catalog
  schema, generated files match the catalog, every pair renders, sealed files hold ciphertext,
  the commit's reservations fit the node.
  - **Proof required:** (1) Offline: each check has a passing fixture and at least one failing fixture per mistake it
    exists for; the chain stops at the first failure and reports that check's sentence; a check that
    crashes counts as a refusal, never as a pass; a test fails if a check is in the list but has no
    fixtures. (2) `orbit check all` on the desk and the gate in orbitd give the same verdict and the
    same sentence for every fixture (one shared list, proven by running both). (3) Live scenario S21:
    for each check, a deliberately bad commit is pushed to the instance repo; pasted for each: orbitd
    refuses it, every pod is unchanged (same pod UIDs before and after), `orbit_deploy_status` shows
    the refusal sentence, the ledger has the entry, `OrbitCommitRefused` fired and was delivered
    (alert-recorder); then a good commit applies and the alert resolves. (4) The gate never blocks
    what it should not: every commit of the instance repo's last 100 passes the chain (run offline,
    tally pasted). (5) A refused commit followed by an orbitd restart still runs the last good commit.
    (6) Cost measured: seconds and peak memory per gate run pasted; orbitd stays inside its limit.
  - Progress (PR "M2c: the commit gate"): the chain is `orbitd/checks`, one module per check, one
    list (`CHAIN` in `checks/__init__.py`), each `check(ctx) -> None | one sentence`; a check that
    raises is a refusal. Seven checks, in order: `core` (the vendored orbit-core files are byte for
    byte the pinned core's; a commit pinned to another core says to apply the platform), `scaffold`
    (every manifest under clusters/ is exactly what `make init` renders; a manifest added by hand
    is refused), `catalog` (the generator accepts the catalog), `generated` (clusters/apps and
    clusters/orbit are what `make sync` writes), `render` (the service chart renders for every
    pair), `sealed` (every sealed secret holds ciphertext; no plain Secret where a hand can put one;
    no credential in a generated manifest), `fit` (project reservations plus the platform's fit the
    node). `core` and `scaffold` were added beyond the first list because the gaps were real (see
    the two §8 rows of 2026-10-06). Both images carry this build's copy of orbit-core
    (`/opt/orbit-core`) and the checks run ONLY that copy's scripts, on a private copy of the
    checkout, with an environment that carries no credential: a commit cannot run code in orbitd.
    Desk: `orbit check --list | all <dir> | <name> <dir>`.
    In orbitd (`orbitd/gate.py`): the chain runs once per new commit; a refused commit is never
    read (the last good commit's tree, written from git, is pinned in its place for every reader of
    desired state); the refusal is kept in the state directory (so a restart knows the last good
    commit), shown as `gate` in `orbit_deploy_status`, written to the audit log, and pushed to
    Alertmanager as `OrbitCommitRefused` until a commit passes; a job waiting for its commit gets
    the failing check's sentence. A check that could not be decided because the cluster did not
    answer refuses nothing for good. Offline proofs (1) and the offline half of (2) and (5):
    `tests/test_checks.py` (27: a passing fixture and a failing one per mistake, on a really
    scaffolded instance; the chain stops at the first failure; a crash is a refusal; a tampered
    script in the commit is never run; a test fails for a check without a failing fixture) and
    `tests/test_gate.py` (9). Live proofs (3) (4) (6) are still to run.
  - Progress 2026-10-06: `scenarios/obs/gate.sh` is the live driver for proof (3): one bad commit per
    check, each confirmed on a scratch copy to stop the desk's chain at exactly its check (core: a
    vendored script edited; scaffold: a rendered manifest edited; catalog: a component kind the
    generator refuses; generated: a catalog change without `make sync`; render: a component named
    `db-backup`, which the generator accepts and the chart refuses; sealed: a plain Secret; fit: 64Gi).
    To run on rc6. The gate also refused a real mistake, unplanned (the rc4 -> rc5 upgrade, §8):
    ```

    00:42:27Z accepted head a86b0e1c8f8f good a86b0e1c8f8f check None |
    ```

  - Progress 2026-10-06 (#241, in v0.34.0-rc6): built. `orbit_db_backup` (operator), `orbit_db_restore`
    (admin; `dry_run` compares and changes nothing), `orbit_project_remove` (admin) run core's own
    scripts in a Job in namespace `orbit` (`orbitd/desk_job.py`: the desk image of the same release,
    the `orbit-platform` ServiceAccount, the checkout at the applied commit, the script from the image
    and never from the instance repository). `scripts/db-backup.sh` is the one backup path: the dump
    and the per-table counts are read in ONE snapshot, the dump is restored into a scratch database on
    the same server and counted, and only an equal restore is a backup; kept on the `orbit-backups`
    volume and copied to the bucket (read back, sha256) when there is one.
    The row's own proof showed a tool was missing (§2: add the tool, do not route around it): S09 and
    S17 cannot run without a shell while a catalog change is a hand edit and a pull request. Added
    `orbit_catalog_set` (operator): one commit by orbitd; `scripts/catalog-edit.py` splices the one
    entry and proves the file then parses to the old catalog with exactly that entry changed; the
    generator judges the entry before the push and the gate judges the commit before anything is
    applied. `orbit_project_remove` deletes the catalog entry the same way, then backs up, then deletes.
    Offline proofs: `tests/test_guardrail_tools.py` and `tests/test_catalog_tool.py` (role refusal,
    nothing without the confirmation, a confirmation not replayable for other arguments or by another
    caller, one audit line per accepted change, every way the script can end);
    `scripts/test_db_backup.py` (16, against a stub that plays a primary: a dump that restores with
    other counts or does not restore is refused and no row is printed; a refused backup refuses the
    restore; bucket keys reach curl on stdin only); `scripts/test_catalog_edit.py`,
    `scripts/test_catalog_commit.py`. Scenarios S07, S09, S17 give the agent no shell; S17b plants a
    dump that cannot be restored and expects nothing deleted. Live proofs: to run on rc6.

    The five that did not, each with its cause (all in §8):
    - S10a: a real gap. The monitor the agent made messaged nobody, and nothing told the agent so.
    - S10b: its setup found the monitors lock held for nine minutes by S10a's delete: a real bug
      (a job waited for the checkout to be exactly at its commit).
    - S17: the driver's own first ship was answered `busy` and it did not ask again (driver).
    - S20: the check's own row failed an honest answer ("I can't say which span took the time").
    - S16: after the reboot everything came back; the row that failed is "no alert firing", and what
      was firing was what the failures above had left on the box:
      ```
        ok    every Deployment and StatefulSet is ready 35 min after boot
        ok    both checkers-lab doors answer 200
        FAIL  firing: KubeJobFailed OrbitIngress5xxRateHigh s10-api-errors s10b-api-errors
        ok    OrbitPlatformMemoryOverBudget never fired (pending or firing) in the 35 min since boot
        ok    a node bootstrap re-run changes nothing (exit 0, k3s already running with current args, not restarted)
        ok    the agent read the platform through orbitd
      5 passed, 1 failed
      ```
      (the two `s10` rules never pruned, the 5xx of the dev api S10b's dead setup left in its fault
      build, and the dev bot's failed Jobs behind that).
    The fixes are one pull request and the next release; then S15, these five and the §6 drivers run
    on it (after a fix only the affected scenarios are re-run, §10).
    One mistake of the driver's own, recorded because it touched the box: the chain was stopped
    while the §6 alerts driver had its faults planted (three throwaway workloads in `monitoring`, a
    112 GB fill file on the node, the dev database fenced, the dev api on its fault build), and a
    stopped driver reverts nothing. Each was put back by hand and checked: the workloads deleted,
    the file removed (`/dev/sda1 150G 21G 124G 15% /`), the database unfenced and ready 1/1, the dev
    api shipped back through orbitd (`DEPLOYED`). A driver is never stopped mid-run again; it is
    left to finish and revert.

    The §6 drivers on v0.34.2 (23:19Z to 03:02Z):

    What failed, and why (the driver's own mistake, written down because it cost a night):
    the afternoon's chain had been "stopped" at 20:02Z, and only its top process had died. Its §6
    drivers went on underneath until 23:25Z (the alerts driver, the watchers, retention, load,
    security: its own summary shows them ending one after another), under the upgrade and under the
    five scenarios above. Worse, at 20:05Z the driver took that run's planted faults for leftovers
    and reverted them by hand while it was alive (its six failed rows). A traffic loop started at
    16:49Z by S10b's dead setup was also still running on the operator machine: a game every 1.7 s
    against the dev api, whose engine (256Mi, 2 MB per game it never frees) ran out of memory every
    few minutes and answered 500, which failed the dev bot's Jobs. That is S16's failed row both
    times (`KubeJobFailed`, `OrbitIngress5xxRateHigh`), the alerts driver's one failed row (the 5xx
    alert was already firing when its errors class began, so no new firing notification), and, with a
    ticker pod the old watchers run left in phase Unknown across the reboot, the watchers' four:
    ```
      ok    errors: OrbitIngress5xxRateHigh is firing in Alertmanager
      FAIL  errors: no firing notification reached the alert-recorder
      ok    errors: OrbitIngress5xxRateHigh resolved after the fault was reverted
      ok    errors: the resolved notification was delivered
      FAIL  loki: the ticker lost lines across the kill (0 of ~79)
      FAIL  alloy: the ticker lost lines across the kill (0 of ~55)
      FAIL  tempo: the ticker lost lines across the kill (0 of ~72)
      FAIL  grafana: the ticker lost lines across the kill (0 of ~55)
    ```
    Everything else passed with that noise running: signals 6, alerts 42 of 43, retention 11, load 4,
    security 7, and the gate's seven cases 61 of 61. The old run was ended between two drivers, the
    loop killed, the box checked clean (no failed Job, no alert but Watchdog, both databases ready).
    v0.34.3 (#257, runner and drivers only): a failed setup runs its teardown, S16 also checks the
    database clusters, the watchers start from a fresh ticker. On it, on the clean box: S15, the
    errors class, the watchers and S16 again.

    The one left is S16 (the reboot). Its "no alert firing" row had passed or failed by the minute

### M3 — Unattended onboarding
- [x] `orbit-onboard` + scripts take the §7 input form at the start, then run with no human: tofu → IP into config automatically → node bootstrap → buckets → platform plan/apply → secrets minted (incl. Grafana admin, renderer token, dev basic-auth) → orbitd bootstrap → `make verify` green.
  - Built (PR v1/m3-onboard-run): `orbit onboard run <dir> --form <form.yaml>` runs the steps config,
    credentials, repository, github-app, infrastructure, node, buckets, sops-key, secrets, platform,
    orbitd-credential, acceptance, recovery-kit in order, each idempotent, values file to file (the form
    must be 0600; a bad field is named, never echoed). Adopting a server needs `hetzner.server_id`, buying
    one needs `create_server: true` (money is a hard stop). `skills/orbit-onboard` leads with it. Offline
    tests `services/orbitd/tests/test_onboard_run.py`. The live proof is M4's rebuild from bare.
  - Evidence (2026-10-08, M4's ninth run: a fresh agent holding orbit-core's skills, a shell and the
    owner's form; core v0.34.17; the test server rebuilt in place). The run's step lines, start to end,
    one `orbit onboard run`, nothing by hand:

    ```
    credentials: created node-key, node-key.pub, ssh-key-path, age.key, env, .sops.yaml

    node: k3s up, kubeconfig written
    ```

    Everything the agent did (its tool calls, in order; the session ran 12:09:37Z to 13:16:36Z, 67
    minutes, of which the run itself took 36):
    ```
    skill orbit-onboard
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && git status -sb | head; git remote -v; ls; ls -la ~/.config/example-platform/; grep -n "name:" instance/config.yaml* | head; date
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && ls instance scripts 2>&1; sed -n 1,15p instance/config.yaml.example; grep -n "core-sync" -A6 Makefile | head -20
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && cat orbit-core.lock; cp instance/config.yaml.example instance/config.yaml && sed -i '9s/name: example /name: example /' instance/c
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && ls scripts | head -30; docker info >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo docker ok; (ORBIT_DESK_PORTS=8765 nohup scripts/desk.sh orbit onboard run /instan
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && sleep 300; tail -n 4 onboard-run.log
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && for i in 1 2; do sleep 280; done; tail -n 3 onboard-run.log
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && sleep 290; sleep 290; tail -n 2 onboard-run.log | cut -c1-300
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && sleep 295; sleep 295; tail -n 3 onboard-run.log | cut -c1-300
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && sleep 295; sleep 295; tail -n 3 onboard-run.log | cut -c1-300
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && date; scripts/desk.sh orbit onboard status /instance --accept 2>&1 | cut -c1-250; echo exit=$?; ls -la ~/.config/example-platform/
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && grep -n FAIL ~/.config/example-platform/acceptance.log | cut -c1-400
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && sed -n 530,540p ~/.config/example-platform/acceptance.log | cut -c1-400; grep -rn "OrbitObjectStoreFailing" --include=*.yaml --inc
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && sed -n 395,445p clusters/platform/monitoring/rules.yaml
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && scripts/desk.sh sh -c 'export KUBECONFIG=/root/.kube/config-example-platform; kubectl get pods -n loki -n tempo 2>&1 | head; kubec
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && scripts/desk.sh make verify > verify2.log 2>&1; echo exit=$?; grep -n "FAIL" verify2.log | cut -c1-300; tail -3 verify2.log | cut -c1-200
    cd ~/source/repos/example-platform && scripts/desk.sh orbit onboard status /instance --accept 2>&1 | grep -E "acceptance|first-project|complete|next" | cut -c1-250; date; git sta
    ```

### M4b — Sizing advice (was: auto-fitting; cut down by the owner, 2026-10-07)
Owner, 2026-10-07, after the research recorded in §10: automatic fitting is NOT built in V1. On one
node with a handful of small services it would free a few hundred MiB, it is the only part of Orbit
that would change running services on its own, and the case it handles worst (a garbage-collected
runtime that fails inside its limit) is the test tenant's own. What V1 keeps is the advice, read-only,
and the one alert the work showed is missing. An agent or a human applies a size with the tool that
already exists (`orbit_catalog_set`), through git and the commit gate. Automatic fitting, the load-test
sizing before a first deploy, per-service floors and ceilings and the Rust decision engine are V2 (§9).
- [x] `orbit_size_recommend` (viewer, read-only): for each component of a project and environment,
  what it asks for, what it used, and a suggested request and limit for memory and CPU with the
  reason in one sentence. The method is the published one (Google's Autopilot moving window, the
  Kubernetes Vertical Pod Autoscaler's defaults): CPU from a high percentile of usage, memory from
  the peaks plus a margin, an out-of-memory kill raises memory, a suggestion within 10% of the
  current value is "leave it". It never suggests less than the highest usage it has seen, says
  "not enough history" instead of guessing, and marks a component whose runtime fills its limit
  (memory flat at the limit, or the runtime's own heap metrics when present) as "memory use does not
  show the need". The skills (`orbit-budget-review`, `orbit-change-service`) say when to ask it and
  how to apply an answer.
  - **Proof required:** (1) Offline, a table-driven test over synthetic series: steady, daily cycle,
    spike, slow leak, an OOM kill, a restart loop, less than a day of history, gaps in the data, usage
    flat at the limit. For each: the suggestion (or the refusal to suggest) is the expected one; no
    suggestion is ever below the series' maximum; no input makes it raise an error. (2) Live, on the
    box: the tool's answer for every checkers-lab component pasted beside the same numbers asked of
    Prometheus directly (requests, limits, peak and percentile over the window); they agree. (3)
    Scenario S22, a fresh operator agent: "is checkers-lab sized sensibly? fix what is clearly wrong"
    with one component deliberately given four times what it uses and one given less than its peak:
    the agent reads the advice, changes those two through `orbit_catalog_set`, leaves the rest, and
    the pods run with the new values; no shell. (4) A viewer token can call it; it writes nothing
    (the audit line says read-only; no commit appears).
  - Evidence (2026-10-10 and 11). (1) Offline: `tests/test_sizing.py` (the table of synthetic
    series, the two invariants, and no input raising), `tests/test_size_recommend_promql.py`
    (every query against hand-computed numbers, by promtool) and
    `tests/test_size_recommend_tool.py` are in the whole orbitd suite: `2512 passed, 13 skipped`
    before v0.34.32.
    (2) Live, the rebuilt box at v0.34.33, 2026-10-10 08:20Z: for every checkers-lab component,
    the tool's line and under it the same numbers asked of Prometheus directly (requests and
    limits from kube-state-metrics, the peak working set and the p95 of CPU over the tool's own
    window; MiB and millicores). They agree to the rounding the tool does (it rounds a peak up):
    ```
    == checkers-lab/prod (namespace checkers-lab-prod, window 7d)
    component              mem req  mem lim  mem peak  cpu req  cpu p95   verdicts (memory / cpu)
    api        tool            256      256       186      250     33.6   lower / lower
               prometheus    256.0    256.0     185.9    250.0     33.6
               memory: peak 186 Mi over 1.7 d plus 15% is 224 Mi, below the current 256 Mi; a runtime that sizes its heap from the limit (.NET, a JVM) gets a smaller heap wi
    engine     tool            256      256       233      250    141.8   no_suggestion / lower
               prometheus    256.0    256.0     232.8    250.0    141.8
               memory: memory use does not show the need: it sits at the limit of 256 Mi without being killed (median 226 Mi, peak 233 Mi), so what it would use with more ro
    == checkers-lab/dev (namespace checkers-lab-dev, window 7d)
    component              mem req  mem lim  mem peak  cpu req  cpu p95   verdicts (memory / cpu)
    api        tool            256      256       211      250     46.2   leave / lower
               prometheus    256.0    256.0     210.1    250.0     46.2

    engine     tool            256      256       256      250    222.0   no_suggestion / leave
               prometheus    256.0    256.0     255.6    250.0    222.0
               memory: memory use does not show the need: it sits at the limit of 256 Mi without being killed (median 253 Mi, peak 256 Mi), so what it would use with more ro
    ```
    (3) S22 of the counted run (v0.34.32), a fresh operator agent, the api given 960Mi (nearly
    four times its use) and the engine 208Mi (less than its peak):

    ```
      ok    the agent asked orbit_size_recommend
    ```

    (4) A viewer can call it and it writes nothing. S06e's agent holds a viewer token; orbitd's
    audit log for its calls, and the instance's main around them:
    ```
    {"actor": "scenario-viewer", "level": "INFO", "logger": "orbitd.audit", "message": "tool orbit_size_recommend ok", "role": "viewer", "time": "2026-10-10T04:24:31.244+00:00", "tool": "orbit_size_recommend", "verdict": "ok"}
    $ git log origin/main --since=2026-10-10T04:22:00Z --until=2026-10-10T04:25:00Z      # the instance repository
    (nothing)
    ```
    **Where this differs from the words of the proof, said plainly:** the proof says "the audit
    line says read-only". An audit line has never carried such a field (it has actor, role,
    tool and verdict), and none was added for this. What shows that the tool writes nothing is
    the absence of any commit around the calls, above, and offline `test_size_recommend_tool.py`,
    which calls it as a viewer and finds the instance repository's HEAD where it was; the tool
    is registered `read_only=True`, which the registry refuses for any tool that returns a
    change.

## 5. Operating scenarios (against checkers-lab)

Each one: a plain-language request to a fresh agent, and a check that decides pass/fail.

| # | Scenario | Pass check |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | Onboard checkers-lab (3 components, DB, dev + prod, domain) from its repo | catalog entry valid, both envs live, verify rows green |
| S2 | Build locally and ship a commit to dev, then promote to prod | deploy status shows the tag on both; post-ship assertions pass |
| S3 | Ship a tag that does not exist | refused before anything changes |
| S4 | Ship a version that crash-loops (`CRASH_ON_START`) | agent detects it and rolls back; prod never served the bad version |
| S5 | Zero-downtime ship, rollback and restart under constant traffic | 0 failed requests; a never-ready version gets no traffic |
| S6 | Diagnose: OOM, 500 burst, slow endpoint, error logs | agent names the cause with metric/log/trace evidence |
| S7 | Bad migration destroys data | agent restores the DB to a point before it (PITR); data back |
| S8 | Add a secret, rotate it, missing-secret startup | app sees the new value; missing secret is diagnosed |
| S9 | Scale, change resources, per-env override, add a domain | live objects match; quota refuses an over-ask |
| S10 | Create a monitor; silence it | alert fires to Telegram; silence suppresses it |
| S11 | Guardrails | viewer token cannot ship; destructive acts need confirmation; replayed confirmation refused; revoked/expired tokens refused |
| S12 | Network isolation | dev cannot reach prod; tenant cannot reach platform internals; undeclared egress blocked |
| S13 | TLS/domains | forced cert renewal works; http→https; dev basic auth 401/200 |
| S14 | Drift | a kubectl hand edit shows in the drift report and is reverted by the next apply |
| S15 | Core upgrade | pin bump → core-sync → apply → verify green, apps uninterrupted |
| S16 | Resilience | reboot: everything recovers, no false alerts; node bootstrap re-run changes nothing |
| S17 | Remove a project | everything it rendered is pruned |
| S18 | Deploy speed | "clean commit → live" measured and recorded here |
| S19 | Switch a feature off and on (admin) | apps gone then back and Ready; ledger holds both; earlier bucket data still there; nothing else changed |
| S20 | Ask for something a disabled feature provides (viewer) | the agent says the feature is off and how to enable it; invents nothing |
| S21 | A bad change is pushed, one per gate check | refused, pods untouched, refusal visible in status, ledger and a delivered alert; the next good commit applies |

New scenarios are added here whenever a mechanism gains a path no scenario exercises.

## 6. Observability suite

1. **Signals end to end** (marker sent, arrival asserted with a time bound): metrics with catalog labels in Prometheus; a unique log line in Loki with correct labels; one trace bot→Traefik→api→engine→Postgres in Tempo; trace↔logs and metric→trace (exemplar) links work in Grafana.
2. **Dashboards**: every panel of the generated dashboards returns data through the Grafana API, no errors, no broken datasource.
3. **Alerts end to end**, one per class — crashloop, OOM, 5xx rate, latency, probe down, cert expiry, disk, node memory, DB down, backup/WAL archive failing, platform memory, orbitd down: fault → pending → firing → Alertmanager → delivered → resolved. Delivery proven by a recording webhook receiver on the box plus Alertmanager's notification counters; the owner glances at Telegram once at the end. Silences work. Every rule has a `promtool test rules` case in CI.
4. **Watching the watchers**: kill each of Prometheus, Loki, Tempo, Alloy, Grafana, Alertmanager → recovers with bounded data loss; bucket unreachable → alert; logs stop arriving → alert; reboot → no false alerts. (External dead-man heartbeat: declined by the owner for V1.)
5. **Retention & storage**: Prometheus 7d honoured and 6-day-old data queryable; Loki/Tempo write to the bucket and expire on schedule; restarts lose no recent data.
6. **Load**: a log burst is ingested or rate-limited with an alert, never silently dropped; a label explosion is caught; the stack's own memory under load stays within budget (measured).
7. **Security**: anonymous Grafana refused; tenants cannot write into the backends; no secrets in alert text or logs.
8. **Agent-facing**: for every fault, the orbitd viewer tools return the evidence a fresh agent needs to name the cause.

## 7. Onboarding input form (given once, at the start)

Hetzner API token · Cloudflare API token + zone · GitHub owner (org or user; the operator's
`gh` must be an admin of it) · node size and location · ACME email · which optional features to
run (none by default; `skills/orbit-onboard` shows what each gives and its measured memory).
Only if a chosen feature needs it: Hetzner S3 access key + secret + location (logs, traces,
backups) · Telegram bot token + chat id (alerts-telegram). Then the two GitHub App clicks.
Nothing else is asked until the run reports green.

## 8. Fixes found along the way

- [x] Memory alerts use working set (includes file cache) → false alarm after reboot (found 2026-10-04). In M0. Fixed in #194; the reboot proof is pending in M0.
  S16 on v0.33.6 (output under M0's memory row): no alert but Watchdog/InfoInhibitor 35 minutes
  after a reboot, and `OrbitPlatformMemoryOverBudget` never pending.
- [x] orbitd `/healthz` reports `0.1.0` (found 2026-10-04). Fixed in #194; on the box v0.33.0 reports `"version":"v0.33.0"`.
- [x] A committed platform pin bump is never applied unless someone runs `orbit platform apply` (orbitd sat at v0.31.6 while git pinned v0.32.1). Record it in the docs and in a verify row that compares the running orbitd image with the pin.
  - PR #194: `make verify` stage6-live: `orbit platform plan` empty, the running orbitd pod is the pin, /healthz reports the pin (run on the box before the release: 1 passed, 2 failed, both reds being this release's own fixes); ORBITD.md says pins are release tags that stage6 checks. Remaining: all three rows green after the release.
  - On v0.33.3 (2026-10-05), `make verify` stage6-live:
    ```
    ok    orbit platform plan is empty — every platform app matches the committed tree
    ok    the running orbitd pod is the pinned image (ghcr.io/yotampeled/orbitd:v0.33.3)
    ok    orbitd's /healthz reports the pinned release (v0.33.3)
    stage6-live: 3 passed, 0 failed
    ```
- [x] `orbit platform plan` never came back empty when an env value was `""` (EnvVar.value is omitempty, so `ORBITD_APPLY_PAIRS=""` read as permanent drift). Fixed in #194 (shadow default + test).
- [x] Main's CI had been red since 2026-09-15 (billing stopped Actions; the guard workflows also ran tests outside pytest). Fixed in #194, see M0.
- [x] Removing a project has no supported path: orbitd prunes only inside a pair that still renders, and the `services` prune refuses Namespaces by design. The esc removal was done by hand (catalog PR, namespaces deleted, `--prune` applies). This is S17's fix (a desk command, not a new orbitd tool). Fixed in #203: `scripts/remove-project.sh`
  (verified pg_dump of every database before any delete, offline tests); proven when S17 passes.
  S17 PASS 5/0 (M2 above): an operator agent removed s17-scratch end to end, `REMOVED: s17-scratch`.
- [x] Shipping a `kind: cron` component always failed the rollout step (it waited for a Deployment). Fixed in #197 (the CronJob template carrying the tag is the landing; pod assertions reported skipped with the reason). Proven on prod with v0.33.0: `rollout ok CronJob checkers-lab-bot ... template=[...:main-0679e4e]`.
- [x] The desk client's 30 s read timeout made every `orbit_job_wait` longer than 30 s a transport failure. Fixed in #197 (150 s read, 15 s connect); the v0.33.0 desk waits through 120 s job_waits.
- [x] An untagged component rendered `:latest`: ImagePullBackOff until the first ship, and a CronJob's first run hung and blocked every later run (concurrencyPolicy Forbid). Fixed in #197 (replicas 0 / suspend: true until a tag exists).
  The chart's idle rule (#197), `scripts/test_service_chart.py` in the desk image, 2026-10-05:
  ```
  scripts/test_service_chart.py::UntaggedComponentTest::test_a_tag_lifts_both PASSED [ 33%]
  scripts/test_service_chart.py::UntaggedComponentTest::test_an_untagged_cronjob_renders_suspended PASSED [ 66%]
  scripts/test_service_chart.py::UntaggedComponentTest::test_an_untagged_deployment_renders_zero_replicas PASSED [100%]
  ======================= 3 passed, 30 deselected in 4.93s =======================
  ```
- [x] A component with no tag was rendered `tag: latest` by the generator, so the chart's idle rule never
  applied (prod's untagged engine/bot pulled `:latest`; a stuck bot Job blocked the CronJob). Fixed in #200
  (no tag written until the first ship).
  The generator change had no test; `scripts/test_catalog_no_tag.py` (this PR) runs the real catalog-sync
  on a catalog with nothing shipped (no component carries a tag) and with one shipped (only it does):
  `2 passed`. Against the old default (`c.get("tag", "latest")`) it fails:
  ```
  E           AssertionError: 'tag' unexpectedly found in {'image': 'example/web', 'kind': 'web', 'memory': '256Mi', 'name': 'web', 'path': '/', 'port': 8080, 'tag': 'latest'} : web: an unshipped component was written with tag 'latest'
  ```
- [x] The derived ResourceQuota left out the barman-cloud sidecar of a WAL-archiving database (192Mi), so
  the first prod ship had no room for its surge pod. Fixed in #200 (read from the chart). Unblocked by hand
  on the box: the stuck Job deleted, the api ReplicaSet recreated after the controller's backoff.
  Proven on v0.33.3 by prod ships with a surge pod beside the WAL-archiving database (S02, S05):
  ```
  ok    prod api runs main-0c245aa
  ok    0 failed of 698 requests under the ship, rollback and restart
  ```
- [x] Tenant probes and stage2 checked `https://<host>` (root) for a project whose only route is `/api`:
  OrbitSiteDown fired on both doors and stage2 read 404. Fixed in #200 (door_path: first web path +
  probePath when nothing serves `/`).
  Proven on v0.33.3: `stage2: 25 passed, 0 failed`, and S01's new project judged at its door:
  ```
  ok    doors: prod 200, dev 401 without credentials, 200 with them
  ok    verify stage2: 26 passed, 0 failed — including checkers-s1's doors
  ```
- [x] `orbit_deploy_status` read every cron component as drift (no pod between runs). Fixed in #200
  (judged on the CronJob template).
  `orbit_deploy_status` for checkers-lab prod on v0.33.6 (2026-10-05), per component:

  ```
  {'component': 'api', 'kind': 'web', 'drift': False}
  {'component': 'engine', 'kind': 'worker', 'drift': False}
  {'component': 'bot', 'kind': 'cron', 'drift': False}
  ```

- [x] `make init` re-runs `core-sync`, which refuses its own uncommitted output: an upgrade needs `make
  core-sync` then `python3 scripts/instance-init.py`. Fix before S15. Fixed in #203 (refuse only when the

- [x] 2026-10-06, found while building the commit gate: an instance never received an update to a
  "static" platform file. Files under `template/clusters/platform` were copied once when the
  instance was created and are not in the vendoring manifest, so `make core-sync` and `make init`
  never touched them again. On the test instance four differed from core at the same pin, two of
  them manifests:
  ```
  differs: platform/apps/blackbox-exporter.yaml
  differs: platform/config/monitoring/netpol.yaml
  ```
  The instance's monitoring netpol lacks the `exporters-ingress` policy core has shipped since the
  area-5 sweep (kube-state-metrics and the blackbox exporter reachable only from `monitoring`; both
  answer any tenant pod today) and still admits a namespace that was deleted in phase 8. Fixed in
  the gate PR: every manifest under `template/clusters/platform` moved into the staged tree, so
  `make init` re-renders all of them on every run and the gate's `scaffold` check holds each one
  byte for byte. Proven when the instance is upgraded to the release carrying it: the two files
  equal core's, the policy exists on the box, a tenant pod cannot reach either exporter, and
  `make verify` and the §6 drivers pass with it.
  - Progress (2026-10-06, test box, instance pinned v0.34.0-rc7): the two files equal core's, the
    policy exists, and a tenant pod reaches neither exporter while Prometheus still does:
    ```
    same as core v0.34.0-rc7: platform/apps/blackbox-exporter.yaml
    same as core v0.34.0-rc7: platform/config/monitoring/netpol.yaml
    $ kubectl -n monitoring get networkpolicy
    exporters-ingress   app.kubernetes.io/name in (blackbox-exporter,kube-state-metrics,prometheus-blackbox-exporter)   11h
    tenant pod checkers-lab-dev/obs-ticker -> kube-prometheus-stack-kube-state-metrics.monitoring.svc:8080/metrics : command terminated with exit code 1
    tenant pod checkers-lab-dev/obs-ticker -> blackbox-exporter.monitoring.svc:9115/metrics : command terminated with exit code 1
    control, same pod -> its own api: (exit 0)
    control, Prometheus (in monitoring): up{job="kube-state-metrics"} => 1 ; probe_success for 4 targets => 1 (answered through the blackbox exporter)
    ```
    Still open: `make verify` and the §6 drivers on the release (the Released row's run).
  - Evidence (2026-10-07): with the two files equal to core's and the policy in place (above),
    `make verify` passes on v0.34.3 (the S15 row: stage2 25, stage3 411, stage4 10, stage5 92,
    stage6 3, stage7 19, all passed) and every §6 driver row has passed on v0.34.2 and v0.34.3
    (the Released row in §4: signals 6, alerts 43, watchers 18, retention 11, load 4, security 7,
    gate 61).

- [x] 2026-10-06, found while building the commit gate: orbitd runs scripts out of the instance
  repository with its own credentials in reach. `scripts/ship.sh` (from the image) runs the CLONE's
  `scripts/bump-and-push.sh`, which runs the clone's `bump-tag.py`; whoever can land a commit on the
  instance repo can therefore run code inside orbitd on the next ship (it holds the GitHub App key,
  the registry token, the token signing key). The new feature switch and the commit gate do not have
  this property (they lay this build's core over the clone first; `test_checks.py` and
  `test_features_commit.py` plant a script that would leave a mark and assert it never runs). Fix:
  the ship path runs this build's `bump-and-push.sh` and `bump-tag.py` (from `/opt/orbit-core`),
  refusing a clone pinned to another core, with the same planted-script test; the gate's `core`
  check already refuses a commit that edits a vendored script, which closes the window from the
  other side once the gate is live.
  - **Proof required:** offline, `scripts/test_ship.py` gains the planted-script case (fails
    before the fix) and the pinned-to-another-core refusal; live, S02 and S18 pass unchanged on the
    release carrying it (a ship still lands and its duration is recorded).
  - Progress (#245, in v0.34.0-rc7): `scripts/ship.sh` with a core of its own runs this build's
    `bump-and-push.sh` on the clone, refuses a clone pinned to another core, and after every reset
    asks `scripts/core-same.py` whether the clone's vendored core is byte for byte the build's.
    `scripts/test_ship_own_scripts.py`: each of the four scripts a ship would run, replaced by one
    that leaves a mark, is refused naming the file, the mark is never made and nothing is pushed; a
    `scripts/yaml.py` planted beside them is refused too (the gate's `core` check gained the same
    rule). Live, on rc7: S02 passed (8 of 8, 2026-10-06 09:48Z, under the tools row in M2c): a
    ship lands through orbitd's own scripts. S18 (the ship's duration) runs with the full
    suite on the release; open until then.
  - Evidence (2026-10-06, v0.34.1, the suite): S02 and S18 pass on a release carrying it; a ship
    lands through orbitd's own scripts and its duration is recorded:
    ```
    == S02-build-ship-promote
      ok    dev api runs main-e56aa0d
      ok    dev engine runs main-e56aa0d
      ok    dev bot's CronJob carries main-e56aa0d
      ok    prod api runs main-e56aa0d
      ok    prod engine runs main-e56aa0d
      ok    prod bot's CronJob carries main-e56aa0d
      ok    the ledger shows dev took main-e56aa0d (2026-10-06T17:26:15Z) before prod (2026-10-06T17:29:19Z)
      ok    a ship reported a DEPLOYED verdict
    8 passed, 0 failed
    == S18-deploy-speed
      ok    dev api runs main-8abb2c6
      ok    dev engine runs main-8abb2c6
      ok    dev bot's CronJob carries main-8abb2c6
      ok    clean commit -> live in dev: 137 s (merge at 17:31:54, api ready at 17:34:11)
    4 passed, 0 failed
    ```

- [x] 2026-10-06, found by running the onboarding form the whole offline way: `orbit onboard run` could
  never have passed `make init`. The filled config still held seven example values the scaffolder
  refuses (`coreRegistry.owner`, the `agentRuntime` and `intakeRuntime` images and tags, the admin's
  first and last name), and nothing sealed `monitoring/alertmanager-intake`, which Alertmanager mounts
  on every platform (its pod would not have started). M4's rebuild would have stopped at the
  infrastructure step. Fixed in #243; the test that found it stays (`tests/test_onboard_features.py`):
  ```
  agentRuntime.image: 'ghcr.io/example-core-org/orbit-agent-runtime'
  agentRuntime.tag: 'main-0000000'
  contacts.adminFirstName: 'Ada'
  contacts.adminLastName: 'Admin'
  coreRegistry.owner: 'ghcr.io/example-core-org'
  intakeRuntime.image: 'ghcr.io/example-core-org/orbit-intake'
  intakeRuntime.tag: 'main-0000000'
  Every line above is a placeholder shipped by instance/config.yaml.example.
  ```
- [x] 2026-10-06, the rc4 -> rc5 upgrade of the test box: its `features:` block named logs and traces
  (the only features when it was written), so the features rc5 split out of core (dashboards, uptime,
  databases, backups, alerts-telegram) read as off. The gate refused the upgrade commit at the catalog
  check (output under M2c's gate row); declaring them with `orbit features set` and a pull request was
  accepted. No released core had a `features:` block, so no instance outside this box can be in that
  state; the CHANGELOG says what an upgrade must name. Also seen: while the gate holds a commit of an
  older core, `orbit_features_set` reads that commit's registry and cannot name the new features, so
  the way out is the desk path (`orbit features set`), as designed for recovery.
- [x] 2026-10-06, core's own rule caught a shortcut: the first cut of the in-cluster point-in-time
  restore copied the bucket Secret between namespaces through the API. `scripts/test_no_secret_reads*.py`
  refused it in CI. The Job is now handed the keys by reference (`orbit/orbit-s3`) and writes the
  throwaway namespace's Secret from its own environment on stdin; nothing reads a Secret.
- [x] 2026-10-06: the generator accepted a component named `db-backup` beside a database and an unknown
  top-level key in a project entry; the chart refused the first (the gate's render check caught it)
  and nothing read the second. Fixed in #245 (in v0.34.0-rc7): both are refused at generation, by
  name. `scripts/test_catalog_features.py::WhatTheGeneratorRefusesByName` (4 cases, the fixtures and
  the template's worked project held to the closed key set); on the real instance the rc7 upgrade ran
  `make sync` and the whole chain:

- [x] 2026-10-06, S07 on rc6: a restore asked for right after its comparison was refused because the
  comparison's throwaway namespace was still terminating (deleted without waiting), after the safety
  backup had been taken. `scripts/pitr-restore.sh` now waits up to 5 minutes for a namespace that is
  on its way out and still leaves one that is not terminating alone (#242). The comparison's job also
  read `failed` in `orbit_job_wait` although its result was `dry_run`: a job whose work was a dry run

- [x] 2026-10-06, seen on the test box while the logs-off proofs ran: `KubeJobFailed` had been firing
  for more than four hours for two Jobs in the `orbit` namespace, both of them guardrail tools doing
  their work: the restore S07 was refused on rc6 and the removal S17b must refuse.
  ```
  NAMESPACE   NAME                             STATUS   COMPLETIONS   DURATION   AGE
  orbit       orbit-desk-db-restore-c7d212be   Failed   0/1           4h40m      4h40m
  orbit       orbit-desk-remove-1386ffff       Failed   0/1           4h24m      4h24m
  firing: KubeJobFailed Watchdog
  ```
  A desk Job is kept for a day after it ends, so every refusal an agent had already been given also
  rang as an alert for a day, and an agent on call would chase it. Fix: once orbitd has read a failed
  desk Job (the caller has the outcome and the script's sentence), it writes the script's last lines
  to its own log and deletes the Job. A Job orbitd never read (it restarted, or gave up waiting)
  stays and alerts, which is what the alert is for.
  - **Proof required:** offline, a test in `services/orbitd/tests/test_guardrail_tools.py` runs a
    refused backup and finds no Job left and the script's lines in orbitd's log, and a second finds
    a Job that succeeded still kept; live, S17b on the release carrying it passes with its new row
    ("no failed removal Job is left behind") and `KubeJobFailed` is not firing afterwards.
  - Evidence (the counted §5 run, the rebuilt box, v0.34.32, 2026-10-10 03:24Z to 08:17Z). Offline, both whole suites before v0.34.32: `scripts suite: 1342 passed, 0 failed`, `2512 passed, 13 skipped`. S17b:

    ```
      ok    the namespace s17b-scratch-dev is still there
      ok    every pod is the same pod as before (3 uid(s))
      ok    the database still holds its 7 notes
      ok    the scratch database of the failed check was dropped
      ok    no file on the backups volume is offered as a backup of s17b-scratch-dev
      ok    no failed removal Job is left behind (the refusal was reported; nothing keeps KubeJobFailed firing)
      ok    the agent used orbitd's tools only (no shell in its transcript)
    10 passed, 0 failed
    ```
    and afterwards: S16, the run's last scenario, `ok    no alert fires but Watchdog/InfoInhibitor`;
    asked of Prometheus at 08:19Z, the firing set was `['Watchdog:firing']`.

  orbitd and sent to nobody (by design: "a monitor its owner wants paged joins the human channel"),
  but no tool description, no answer and no skill said that `notify` exists; an agent could only
  guess it. (2) The check: it counted every Telegram notification since the scenario began, so
  another alert's notification made it pass. The earlier passes of S10a (2026-10-05) were of this kind:
  that transcript never mentions `notify` either. Fix: the tool's description and the operator skill
  name `notify` and what leaving it out means; every answer of `orbit_monitor_upsert` carries
  `evidence.reaches` and says it in the `validate` step; S10a's check waits for the delivery
  recorder's line naming this monitor.

- [x] 2026-10-06, found behind S10a and S10b in the suite on v0.34.1: a job that waits for "its" commit
  fails when another commit follows within a minute. S10a's teardown deleted its monitor and, 15 s
  later, shipped the dev api back. orbitd's log:
  ```
  16:46:35 deploy orbit_monitor_delete monitors/ s10-api-errors -: running
  16:46:46 deploy orbit_deploy_ship checkers-lab/dev api main-dd10411: running
  16:47:17 commit 1139326f2bbd accepted by the gate
  16:49:53 deploy orbit_monitor_upsert monitors/ s10b-api-errors -: busy
  16:55:35 deploy orbit_monitor_delete monitors/ s10-api-errors -: failed
  reconcile monitors would prune (never deleted here): monitoring.coreos.com/v1/PrometheusRule/monitoring/orbit-monitor-s10-api-errors, monitoring.coreos.com/v1/PrometheusRule/monitoring/orbit-monitor-s10b-api-errors
  ```
  The checkout follows the repository about once a minute, so it went from before the delete's
  commit (4179e2c, 16:46:37) straight to the ship's (1139326, 16:46:52); the delete's job waited for
  HEAD to BE 4179e2c, held the monitors lock for its whole nine-minute deadline (S10b's setup found
  it busy and died), then failed, and its rule was never pruned. The rule `orbit-monitor-s10b-api-errors`
  was created on 2026-10-05: the same thing had happened two days earlier and nothing had shown it.
  Every job that commits waits this way (ship, rollback, restart, monitor upsert and delete, catalog
  set, feature set), and two agents working in the same minute is the ordinary case. Fix: (1) the wait
  ends when the checkout carries the commit (it is the commit or comes after it, asked of the
  checkout's history); (2) `orbit_monitor_delete` on a name gone from the repository whose objects
  are still there confirms naming them and prunes them; (3) the scenario runner fails a scenario
  whose teardown fails, and S10's teardown checks the rule is gone.
  - **Proof required:** offline, `tests/test_deploy_wait.py` (a job whose commit was followed by
    another does not wait; a checkout that never gets the commit is still lag; ancestry both ways)
    and `tests/test_monitor_tools.py` (what an unfinished delete left behind is cleaned by deleting
    again, after a confirmation that names it; the monitor beside it stays). Live, on the release
    carrying it: the two rules left on the box are removed by `orbit_monitor_delete` (output
    pasted, `kubectl get prometheusrule` shows neither); S10a and S10b pass with their teardown's
    "the monitor's rule is gone from the cluster".
  - Evidence (2026-10-06, v0.34.2). The two rules left on the box, removed by `orbit_monitor_delete`:
    ```
    22:07:25Z calling orbit_monitor_delete {"name": "s10-api-errors"}
    22:07:42Z job completed
    elapsed_s 16 status applied commit None
      step confirm ok
      step commit skipped the file is already gone from the repository
      step prune ok 1 deleted, 0 absent
      evidence objects = ["PrometheusRule/monitoring/orbit-monitor-s10-api-errors"]
      evidence left_behind = true
    22:07:42Z calling orbit_monitor_delete {"name": "s10b-api-errors"}
    22:07:58Z job completed
    elapsed_s 16 status applied commit None
      step confirm ok
      step commit skipped the file is already gone from the repository
      step prune ok 1 deleted, 0 absent
      evidence objects = ["PrometheusRule/monitoring/orbit-monitor-s10b-api-errors"]
      evidence left_behind = true
    rules named orbit-monitor-s10* left in the cluster: 0
    ```
    S10a and S10b: each teardown deletes its monitor and ships the dev api back 15 s later, the
    sequence that failed on v0.34.1; both deletes pruned (S10a 5 of 5, S10b 2 of 2, above under the
    Released row):
    ```
    S10a teardown: the monitor's rule is gone from the cluster
    S10b teardown: the monitor's rule is gone from the cluster
    ```

- [x] 2026-10-07, S16 (the reboot) on v0.34.3: three hours after the reboot `KubeJobFailed` was still
  firing for the checkers-lab bot's run of 10:00Z, in dev and in prod, the run the reboot cut:
  ```
  NAMESPACE           NAME                        STATUS   COMPLETIONS   DURATION   AGE
  checkers-lab-dev    checkers-lab-bot-29856120   Failed   0/1           3h11m      3h11m
  checkers-lab-prod   checkers-lab-bot-29856120   Failed   0/1           3h11m      3h11m
        2 KubeJobFailed
  ```
  Every run after it succeeded. The chart keeps a cron's last three failed Jobs
  (`failedJobsHistoryLimit: 3`) and nothing else removes one, so a single failed run rings until
  three later runs fail too: for a healthy cron, for ever. Whether S16's "no alert firing" row passed
  had depended on the minute of the reboot. Fix: a finished run's Job is deleted after 20 minutes
  (the alert waits 15), so one failed run is told once and clears, and a cron that keeps failing
  keeps a failed Job in sight. The watchers driver's ticker pod, which its own reboot leaves in
  phase Unknown, is now removed by force.
  - **Proof required:** offline, `scripts/test_service_chart.py` (the rendered CronJob carries
    `ttlSecondsAfterFinished: 1200`; a component may set its own). Live, on the release carrying
    it: the bot's CronJobs on the box carry it; S16 passes every row, with the reboot made at a
    cron tick (the scenario's reboot within the minute of a scheduled run), pasted.
  - Evidence (the counted §5 run, the rebuilt box, v0.34.32, 2026-10-10 03:24Z to 08:17Z). Offline, both whole suites before v0.34.32: `scripts suite: 1342 passed, 0 failed`, `2512 passed, 13 skipped`. The bot's CronJobs on the box:
    ```
    checkers-lab-dev/checkers-lab-bot ttl=1200
    checkers-lab-prod/checkers-lab-bot ttl=1200
    ```
    S16, with the reboot made while a bot run was in flight:
    ```
    rebooting 9 s after a ten-minute mark (a bot run is in flight)

    deployments not ready after the wait: 0
    orbitd's door answers: https://orbit.example.com/healthz 200 (3 in a row)
      (waiting 2000s until 35 minutes after boot)
      ok    every Deployment and StatefulSet is ready 35 min after boot
      ok    every database cluster has all its instances ready
      ok    both checkers-lab doors answer 200
      ok    no alert fires but Watchdog/InfoInhibitor
      ok    OrbitPlatformMemoryOverBudget never fired (pending or firing) in the 35 min since boot

      ok    the agent read the platform through orbitd
    ```

- [x] 2026-10-07, the registry row's last live proof (databases off and on, with no database project
  on the box): `orbit_features_set databases off` never converged. The commit landed and the retire
  of the operator was refused, every time:
  ```
  14:30:55Z calling orbit_features_set databases off
  {"action": "retire", "app": "cloudnative-pg", "error": "orbit platform retire: refusing cloudnative-pg: deleting this set would garbage-collect Secret cnpg-system/cnpg-ca-secret, Secret cnpg-system/cnpg-webhook-cert outside the Namespaces this run deletes", "for": ["databases"], "ok": false}], "failed": ["databases"]}, "verdict": "LANDED NOT CONVERGED"
  ```
  The two Secrets are the operator's own certificates, owned by its Deployment
  (`owners=Deployment/cloudnative-pg`), and the guard that keeps a retire from collecting a Secret it
  does not own is right to refuse what it cannot tell apart. `orbit_features_list` said so the whole
  time ("off, STILL APPLIED"); the first attempt of the day had gone by unseen because the driver's
  helper printed `status None` for a failed job. Fix: a feature names the Secrets its apps make for
  themselves (`generated:` in the registry; databases names these two); a retire lets exactly
  those go and says so; any other Secret is still a refusal of the whole run. The helper now
  requires each switch to end applied.
  - **Proof required:** offline, `tests/test_platform_retire.py` (the named Secret goes and is said;
    a second, unnamed one still refuses everything; a Secret named under another app's feature is
    not this app's to let go). Live, on the release carrying it: databases off (the operator's
    namespace has no workload, `orbit_features_list` says off and not applied) and on again (the
    operator ready), with the platform pictured before and after each, pasted under the registry row.
  - Evidence (2026-10-08). Offline, at v0.34.10 (`bash scripts/local-tests.sh orbitd
    tests/test_platform.py tests/test_platform_retire.py`):


- [x] 2026-10-08 00:20Z, M4's second live run (a fresh agent, v0.34.11): past the form
  (`private: made readable by its owner alone: example-platform, github-app.json`), then:
  ```
  config: wrote git.repoUrl, git.github_org, domains.primary, domains.dev, domains.dnsZones, registry, ...
  credentials: created node-key, node-key.pub, ssh-key-path, age.key, env, .sops.yaml
  repository: STOPPED — git push to the instance repository failed
  ```
  The agent read the refusal ("Changes must be made through a pull request") and stopped, rightly:
  nothing on the server or the DNS had changed. The test organization's repository has carried a
  ruleset since before V1 (`protected lines: main and dev`: no deletion, no force push, pull requests
  only; bypass: the GitHub App of the platform before the rebuild). Two things follow. (1) The run
  pushes its three commits straight to main, which a repository like this refuses: the run could
  only ever onboard a repository nobody had protected, and a rebuild after a disaster is exactly the
  case where it is protected. (2) Orbit writes to main as its App (the catalog, the features, every
  deploy), and the rebuild makes a new App: with only the old App on the bypass list the platform
  would have come up and failed at its first write. Fixed in v0.34.12: a push main refuses for wanting
  a pull request goes through one (a branch, a pull request, a merge, the checkout follows; the
  rule is kept, not worked around); the `github-app` step adds the App to the bypass list of each
  active repository ruleset that covers main and blocks a push, changes nothing else in it,
  removes nobody, and says which ruleset; a rule on the organization, or a pull request that wants
  a review, is a stop in one sentence. Orbit does not create such a rule itself: whether it should
  (so that main is protected on every instance from the first minute) is a V2 question, §9.
  - **Proof required:** offline, `tests/test_onboard_run.py` (a main that takes pull requests
    only gets the commit through one; a push main takes needs none, and any other refusal is a
    stop; a pull request that cannot be merged stops in one sentence; the App is added to a rule
    that keeps pushes off main and nothing else changes; rules that do not keep the App off main
    are left alone; a rule on the organization is not the run's to change). Live: M4's run passes
    `repository` and `github-app` on the test organization's repository with its ruleset in place,
    the ruleset afterwards lists the new App and is otherwise unchanged, and orbitd's first write to
    main after the rebuild (a feature switch or S01's project) lands.
  - Evidence (2026-10-08). Offline (`... orbitd`, the whole suite at the change): `2493 passed, 13
    skipped`. Live, the ninth run: `repository` and `github-app` pass with the ruleset in place

  stop names it.

  ended `ok`; two applies side by side is what §2 says never to do, and the agent's pass alone

- [x] 2026-10-08, read in the code before M4's restore (the row "Restore checkers-lab's data from
  the bucket"): `orbit_db_restore` could not do it. Every backup leaves a copy in the bucket
  (`orbit-backups/<namespace>/<file>`, read back and compared), and the restore looked for the file
  on the platform's backups volume and nowhere else:
  ```
  FILE="$DIR/$FROM"
  [ -f "$FILE" ] || refuse "there is no backup $FROM of $NS/$C on this platform"
  ```
  That volume belongs to the node. On the day the bucket copy is the only one left (the node
  rebuilt or lost, which is what M4 is), the tool that restores a database had no way to reach it,
  and the way round would have been a desk script that skips the confirmation, the pre-restore
  backup and the ledger: the thing §2 says never to do. Fixed in v0.34.19: a named backup that is not
  on the volume is fetched from the bucket (the keys to curl on stdin, as the backup does), checked
  as a gzip file, kept on the volume, and restored as any other; `latest` is the newest name of
  the volume's and the bucket's; what is in neither place is refused in one sentence that names
  both.
  - **Proof required:** offline, `scripts/test_db_backup.py` (a rebuilt platform restores from the
    bucket by name and by `latest`; a backup on the volume is used without asking the bucket; what
    is in neither place, or is not a gzip file in the bucket, is refused and nothing is touched; no
    key on a command line). Live: M4's restore row, done with `orbit_db_restore` and nothing else,
    its job's output showing `fetched from the bucket:`.
  - Evidence (2026-10-08). Offline (`... scripts scripts/test_db_backup.py`, at the change): `19
    passed in 14.92s`; the whole orbitd suite: `2501 passed, 13 skipped`. Live: M4's restore row,
    every restore by `orbit_db_restore`:
    ```
      | == checkers-lab-db-20261007T133318Z.sql.gz is not on this platform's volume: fetching s3://example-orbit-platform/orbit-backups/checkers-lab-dev/checkers-lab-db-20261007T133318Z.sql.gz
      | fetched from the bucket: s3://example-orbit-platform/orbit-backups/checkers-lab-dev/checkers-lab-db-20261007T133318Z.sql.gz sha256 b92fc561083231d1
      step restore ok RESTORED: checkers-lab-prod/checkers-lab-db holds the data of checkers-lab-db-20261007T133320Z.sql.gz
      | == checkers-lab-db-20261007T133320Z.sql.gz is not on this platform's volume: fetching s3://example-orbit-platform/orbit-backups/checkers-lab-prod/checkers-lab-db-20261007T133320Z.sql.gz
    ```
